stability of(D,c)(D,c)-tuples (Claims7.24and7.25) keeps the minus-parts
controlled. This yields (7.31) and (7.32), and hence, by the criterion (7.17), an
elementgp′∈G^1​(Y,p​x)g^{\prime}_{p}\in\widehat{G}_{1}(Y,px)withd​(gp′,p​g​p−1)≤c−1​(κ+ϵ)1/2​R2​nd(g^{\prime}_{p},pgp^{-1})\leq c^{-1}(\kappa+\epsilon)^{1/2}R^{2n}.

Section7.3.3globalizes this local step. Applying Claim7.20to the points of aK−dim(G)−1K^{-\dim(G)^{-1}}-net{gk}k=1K⊂G1\{g_{k}\}_{k=1}^{K}\subset G_{1}, Claim7.27produces, for mostpp, a net contained inG^1​(YR​(f),p​x)\widehat{G}_{1}(Y_{R}(f),px). The proof of Lemma7.13then
transfers this conclusion from points of the formp​xpxback to a large subset ofXX, again via Fubini and theϵ\epsilon-PP-almost invariance ofλ\lambda(Equations (7.36) and (7.37)). Optimizing the parameters by takingc=R−2​nc=R^{-2n}andK=Rn/2K=R^{n/2}gives the final net radius2​R−n2​dim(G)+(κ+ϵ)​R4​n2R^{-\frac{n}{2}\dim(G)}+(\kappa+\epsilon)R^{4n}and the stated lower bound on the
measure of the good set.

## 7.3.1What exactly isG^1​(YR​(f),x)\hat{G}_{1}(Y_{R}(f),x)?

In this part we give an explicit easy condition for belonging to the setG^1​(YR​(f),x)\hat{G}_{1}(Y_{R}(f),x).
The following two definitions are a crucial part of the terminology required for this task.

## Definition 7.16.

SupposeV:=G+,stabG⁡(x)∩GR||V:=G_{+},\operatorname{stab}_{G}(x)\cap G_{R}^{||}generateGGinnnsteps, then for everyg∈G1g\in G_{1}there areγ1​(g,x),…,γn​(g,x)∈stabG⁡(x)∩GR||\gamma_{1}(g,x),\dots,\gamma_{n}(g,x)\in\operatorname{stab}_{G}(x)\cap G_{R}^{||}such thatg∈γ1​(g,x)​V1​γ2​(g,x)​V1​⋯​γn​(g,x)​V1.g\in\gamma_{1}(g,x)V_{1}\gamma_{2}(g,x)V_{1}\cdots\gamma_{n}(g,x)V_{1}.(7.12)

Define, for eachk=1,…,nk=1,\dots,n, the maps (which are polynomial in the entries of the variables):ψk,g,x:V1×⋯×V1⏟k→G,ρk,g,x:V1×⋯×V1⏟k→G\psi_{k,g,x}:\underbrace{V_{1}\times\cdots\times V_{1}}_{k}\rightarrow G,\rho_{k,g,x}:\underbrace{V_{1}\times\cdots\times V_{1}}_{k}\rightarrow G(7.13)

byψk,g,x​(v1,…,vk)=γ1​(g,x)​v1​⋯​γk​(g,x)​vk​γk+1​(g,x)\psi_{k,g,x}(v_{1},\dots,v_{k})=\gamma_{1}(g,x)v_{1}\cdots\gamma_{k}(g,x)v_{k}\gamma_{k+1}(g,x)

andρk,g,x​(v1,…,vk)=γ1​(g,x)​v1​⋯​γk​(g,x)​vk.\rho_{k,g,x}(v_{1},\dots,v_{k})=\gamma_{1}(g,x)v_{1}\cdots\gamma_{k}(g,x)v_{k}.

Moreover, for every tuple(v1,…,vn)(v_{1},\dots,v_{n})for which they are defined, defineψk,g,x+​(v1,…,vk)=ψk,g,x​(v1,…,vk)+\psi_{k,g,x}^{+}(v_{1},\dots,v_{k})=\psi_{k,g,x}(v_{1},\dots,v_{k})_{+}and similarlyρk,g,x+​(v1,…,vk)=ρk,g,x​(v1,…,vk)+\rho_{k,g,x}^{+}(v_{1},\dots,v_{k})=\rho_{k,g,x}(v_{1},\dots,v_{k})_{+}whereg+g_{+}is defined as in Definition7.2.
Similarly, we defineψk,g,x−​(v1,…,vk),ρk,g,x−​(v1,…,vk)\psi_{k,g,x}^{-}(v_{1},\dots,v_{k}),\rho_{k,g,x}^{-}(v_{1},\dots,v_{k}).

The next definition is used to induce conditions on theG+,G−G_{+},G_{-}parts of intermediate products ofV1V_{1}and elements fromstabG⁡(x)\operatorname{stab}_{G}(x).

## Proof.

We know thatλ​(X′′)≥1−κ\lambda(X^{\prime\prime})\geq 1-\kappaand that sinceλ\lambdaisϵ\epsilon-norm-PP-invariant, we have for everyv∈VRn||v\in V_{R^{n}}^{||}that∥v.λ−λ∥T​V≤ϵRn\left\lVert v.\lambda-\lambda\right\rVert_{TV}\leq\epsilon R^{n}.

By Fubini/Markov this implies thatλ(x:mVRn||(ox(v)∈X′′)≥(1−κ−ϵRn)1/2≥(1−κ−ϵRn)1/2\lambda(x:m_{V_{R^{n}}^{||}}(o_{x}(v)\in X^{\prime\prime})\geq(1-\kappa-\epsilon R^{n})^{1/2}\geq(1-\kappa-\epsilon R^{n})^{1/2}and we defineX′′′X^{\prime\prime\prime}to be this set, namelyX′′′={x:mVRn||​(ox​(v)∈X′′)≥(1−κ−ϵ​Rn)1/2}.\displaystyle X^{\prime\prime\prime}=\{x:m_{V_{R^{n}}^{||}}(o_{x}(v)\in X^{\prime\prime})\geq(1-\kappa-\epsilon R^{n})^{1/2}\}.

and similarly for everyx∈X′′′x\in X^{\prime\prime\prime}mVRn||(ox(v)∈X′′)≥(1−κ−ϵRn)1/2)≥1−(κ+ϵ)1/2Rn.\displaystyle m_{V_{R^{n}}^{||}}(o_{x}(v)\in X^{\prime\prime})\geq(1-\kappa-\epsilon R^{n})^{1/2})\geq 1-(\kappa+\epsilon)^{1/2}R^{n}.(7.25)

IntersectingX′′′X^{\prime\prime\prime}withX′′X^{\prime\prime}, which has measure at least1−κ1-\kappa, we may assume thatX′′′⊂X′′X^{\prime\prime\prime}\subset X^{\prime\prime}and in addition to Equation (7.25) we also haveλ​(X′′′)≥1−(κ+ϵ)1/2​Rn−κ≥1−2​(κ+ϵ)1/2​Rn\lambda(X^{\prime\prime\prime})\geq 1-(\kappa+\epsilon)^{1/2}R^{n}-\kappa\geq 1-2(\kappa+\epsilon)^{1/2}R^{n}as desired.

## Proof of Claim7.20.

The proof has two main steps.

Step 1: turning the tuple(p​γ1​p−1,p​v1​p−1,…,p​γn​p−1,p​vn​p−1)(p\gamma_{1}p^{-1},pv_{1}p^{-1},\dots,p\gamma_{n}p^{-1},pv_{n}p^{-1})into a(D,c)(D,c)-tuple:Applying Claim7.24to the tuple(γ1,v1,…,γn,vn)(\gamma_{1},v_{1},\dots,\gamma_{n},v_{n}), we obtain that for everyppin the subset𝒫+\mathcal{P}_{+}defined in the claim, which has measure at least1−RqG​n​cqG′1-R^{q_{G}n}c^{q_{G}^{\prime}}, the tuple(p​γ1​p−1,p​v1​p−1,…,p​γn​p−1,p​vn​p−1)(p\gamma_{1}p^{-1},pv_{1}p^{-1},\dots,p\gamma_{n}p^{-1},pv_{n}p^{-1})is a(D,c)(D,c)-tuple.
For simplicity of notation, we replaceRRandccbyRqG,cqG′R^{q_{G}},c^{q_{G}^{\prime}}respectively.
Indeed, the conditions of the claim hold forg1=γ1,g2=v1,…,g2​n−1=γn,g2​n=vng_{1}=\gamma_{1},g_{2}=v_{1},\dots,g_{2n-1}=\gamma_{n},g_{2n}=v_{n}which all belong toGR||G_{R}^{||}(theviv_{i}’s even belong toG1G_{1}!).
We thus defineP1​(g,x)=𝒫+P_{1}(g,x)=\mathcal{P}_{+}(recall that𝒫+\mathcal{P}_{+}indeed depends onxxbecause of theγ\gamma’s and onggbecauseg=ρ​(v1,…,vn)g=\rho(v_{1},\dots,v_{n})).

Step 2: wiggling theviv_{i}s so thatρ\rholands intoG^1​(YR​(f),p​x)\hat{G}_{1}(Y_{R}(f),px):By the previous step, for everyp∈P1​(g,x)p\in P_{1}(g,x)the tuple(γ1​(p),v1​(p),…,γn​(p),vn​(p)):=(p​γ1​p−1,p​v1​p−1,…,p​γn​p−1,p​vn​p−1)\displaystyle(\gamma_{1}(p),v_{1}(p),\dots,\gamma_{n}(p),v_{n}(p)):=(p\gamma_{1}p^{-1},pv_{1}p^{-1},\dots,p\gamma_{n}p^{-1},pv_{n}p^{-1})

is a(D,c)(D,c)-tuple.

By DefinitionA.12this means that we have‖Dγ1​(p)​v1​(p),γ2​(p)−1‖≥c\left\lVert D^{-1}_{\gamma_{1}(p)v_{1}(p),\gamma_{2}(p)}\right\rVert\geq cwhere we recall that for everyg1,g2∈Gg_{1},g_{2}\in Gandv0∈V1v_{0}\in V_{1},Dg1,g2​(v0):𝔳→𝔳D_{g_{1},g_{2}}(v_{0}):\mathfrak{v}\to\mathfrak{v}denotes the differential atv0v_{0}of the mapv↦(g1​g2)+−1​(g1​v​g2)+\displaystyle v\mapsto(g_{1}g_{2})_{+}^{-1}(g_{1}vg_{2})_{+}(7.26)

and also means that|(g1​g2)+|≤c−1​|g1​g2|.\displaystyle|(g_{1}g_{2})_{+}|\leq c^{-1}|g_{1}g_{2}|.(7.27)

Define the mapζ1\zeta_{1}byV1∋v↦(γ1​(p)​v​γ2​(p))+∈VRn||.\displaystyle V_{1}\ni v\mapsto(\gamma_{1}(p)v\gamma_{2}(p))_{+}\in V^{||}_{R^{n}}.

Since by the definition of(D,c)(D,c)-tuples we know that‖Dγ1​(p)​v1​(p),γ2​(p)−1​(⋅)‖−1≥c\left\lVert D^{-1}_{\gamma_{1}(p)v_{1}(p),\gamma_{2}(p)}(\cdot)\right\rVert^{-1}\geq cwe can obtain(ζ1)∗​mV1≤‖Dγ1​(p)​v1​(p),γ2​(p)−1‖​mVRn||≤c−1​mVRn||​and​|(γ1​(p)​v1​(p)​γ2​(p))+|≤c−1​|γ1​(p)​v1​(p)​γ2​(p)|.\displaystyle(\zeta_{1})_{*}m_{V_{1}}\leq\left\lVert D^{-1}_{\gamma_{1}(p)v_{1}(p),\gamma_{2}(p)}\right\rVert m_{V_{R^{n}}^{||}}\leq c^{-1}m_{V_{R^{n}}^{||}}\text{ and }|(\gamma_{1}(p)v_{1}(p)\gamma_{2}(p))_{+}|\leq c^{-1}|\gamma_{1}(p)v_{1}(p)\gamma_{2}(p)|.(7.28)

Thus, by Equation (7.28) we obtainmV1​(ζ1−1​(Ep​x))≤c−1​mVRn||​(Ep​x)≤c−1​(κ+ϵ)1/2​Rn:=δ′′.\displaystyle m_{V_{1}}(\zeta_{1}^{-1}(E_{px}))\leq c^{-1}m_{V_{R^{n}}^{||}}(E_{px})\leq c^{-1}(\kappa+\epsilon)^{1/2}R^{n}:=\delta^{\prime\prime}.(7.29)

This means that we can findv∈Vδ′′v\in V_{\delta^{\prime\prime}}such thatv​v1​(p)vv_{1}(p)is in the complement, namelyζ1​(v​v1​(p))∉Ep​x\zeta_{1}(vv_{1}(p))\notin E_{px}. Denotev1′​(p)=v​v1​(p)v_{1}^{\prime}(p)=vv_{1}(p).

we obtainv2′​(p)=v​v2​(p)v_{2}^{\prime}(p)=vv_{2}(p)for somev∈Vδ′′v\in V_{\delta^{\prime\prime}}(possibly different than the one used inζ1\zeta_{1}) such thatζ2​(v2′)∉Ep​x\zeta_{2}(v_{2}^{\prime})\notin E_{px}as well.

we obtain a(D,c/2)(D,c/2)-tuple(γ1​(p),v1′​(p),…,γn​(p),vn′​(p))(\gamma_{1}(p),v_{1}^{\prime}(p),\dots,\gamma_{n}(p),v_{n}^{\prime}(p))such thatd​(vi′​(p),vi​(p))≤δ′′d(v_{i}^{\prime}(p),v_{i}(p))\leq\delta^{\prime\prime}and for everyj=1,…,nj=1,\dots,nwe haveζj​(vj′​(p))∉Ep​x\zeta_{j}(v_{j}^{\prime}(p))\notin E_{px}which means thatψ​(v1′​(p),…,vj′​(p))=(γ1​(p)​v1′​(p)​⋯​vj′​(p)​γj+1​(p))+∉Ep​x.\displaystyle\psi(v_{1}^{\prime}(p),\dots,v_{j}^{\prime}(p))=(\gamma_{1}(p)v_{1}^{\prime}(p)\cdots v_{j}^{\prime}(p)\gamma_{j+1}(p))_{+}\notin E_{px}.(7.31)

and definegp′:=γ1​(p)​v1′​(p)​⋯​γn​(p)​vn′​(p)​and​gp=p​g​p−1=γ1​(p)​v1​(p)​⋯​γn​(p)​vn​(p)\displaystyle g_{p}^{\prime}:=\gamma_{1}(p)v_{1}^{\prime}(p)\cdots\gamma_{n}(p)v_{n}^{\prime}(p)\text{ and }g_{p}=pgp^{-1}=\gamma_{1}(p)v_{1}(p)\cdots\gamma_{n}(p)v_{n}(p)

Define the set𝒟={x∈X:G^1​(Y,x)​contains a2​K−dim(G)−1+c−1​(κ+ϵ)1/2​R2​n-net inG1}.\displaystyle\mathcal{D}=\{x\in X:\hat{G}_{1}(Y,x)\text{ contains a $2K^{-\dim(G)^{-1}}+c^{-1}(\kappa+\epsilon)^{1/2}R^{2n}$-net in $G_{1}$}\}.

Therefore we can continue the estimation above as followsλ​(𝒟)≥∫X′′′mP1​(PK​(x))​𝑑λ​(x)−ϵ−3​(κ+ϵ)1/2​Rn\displaystyle\lambda(\mathcal{D})\geq\int_{X^{\prime\prime\prime}}m_{P_{1}}(P_{K}(x))d\lambda(x)-\epsilon-3(\kappa+\epsilon)^{1/2}R^{n}≥1−K​c​Rn−ϵ−4​(κ+ϵ)1/2​Rn.\displaystyle\geq 1-KcR^{n}-\epsilon-4(\kappa+\epsilon)^{1/2}R^{n}.

andK​c​Rn+ϵ+4​(κ+ϵ)1/2​Rn≤R−n/2+5​(κ+ϵ)1/2​Rn.\displaystyle KcR^{n}+\epsilon+4(\kappa+\epsilon)^{1/2}R^{n}\leq R^{-n/2}+5(\kappa+\epsilon)^{1/2}R^{n}.(7.39)

## Proposition 7.28.

## 8Almost invariant measures and almost projective factors

In this section we prove our main theorem1.10.
The proof begins with the following lemma, helping us to pass (in terms of preserving integrals) from a test function to what will later be a factor function.

## Lemma 8.1.

SupposeXXis aGG-space and thatλ\lambdais a normϵ\epsilon-PP-invariant measure onXXand letu¯θ∈U¯θ\overline{u}_{\theta}\in\overline{U}_{\theta}.
Then for everyT>0T>0large, and every11-Lipschitz functionϕ\phi, we have:∫Xϕf​(u¯θ​x)​𝑑λ​(x)=∫XℰsθT​ϕf​(u¯θ​x)​𝑑λ​(x)+O​(|u¯θ|​T​ϵ).\int_{X}\phi_{f}(\overline{u}_{\theta}x)d\lambda(x)=\int_{X}\mathcal{E}_{s_{\theta}}^{T}\phi_{f}(\overline{u}_{\theta}x)d\lambda(x)+O(\lvert\overline{u}_{\theta}\rvert T\epsilon).(8.1)

## Proof.

Sincesθs_{\theta}commutes withU¯θ\overline{U}_{\theta}(by definition), if we define the functionϕf,u¯θ​(x)=ϕf​(u¯θ​x)\phi_{f,\overline{u}_{\theta}}(x)=\phi_{f}(\overline{u}_{\theta}x)then:ℰsθT​ϕf​(u¯θ​x)=ℰsθT​ϕf,u¯θ​(x).\mathcal{E}_{s_{\theta}}^{T}\phi_{f}(\overline{u}_{\theta}x)=\mathcal{E}_{s_{\theta}}^{T}\phi_{f,\overline{u}_{\theta}}(x).(8.2)

Since the measureλ\lambdaisϵ\epsilon-PP-almost invariant, and since the functionϕf,u¯θ\phi_{f,\overline{u}_{\theta}}is|u¯θ|\lvert\overline{u}_{\theta}\rvert-Lipschitz, we know that:∫XℰsθT​ϕf,u¯θ​(x)​𝑑λ​(x)\displaystyle\int_{X}\mathcal{E}_{s_{\theta}}^{T}\phi_{f,\overline{u}_{\theta}}(x)d\lambda(x)=∫X1T​(∫0Tϕf,u¯θ​(sθt​x)​𝑑t)​𝑑λ​(x)\displaystyle=\int_{X}\frac{1}{T}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\phi_{f,\overline{u}_{\theta}}(s_{\theta}^{t}x)dt\right)d\lambda(x)=1T​∫0T(∫Xϕf,u¯θ​(sθt​x)​𝑑λ​(x))​𝑑t\displaystyle=\frac{1}{T}\int_{0}^{T}\left(\int_{X}\phi_{f,\overline{u}_{\theta}}(s_{\theta}^{t}x)d\lambda(x)\right)dt=∫Xϕf,u¯θ​(x)​𝑑λ​(x)+O​(|u¯θ|​T​ϵ).\displaystyle=\int_{X}\phi_{f,\overline{u}_{\theta}}(x)d\lambda(x)+O(\lvert\overline{u}_{\theta}\rvert T\epsilon).

By definition ofϕf,u¯θ\phi_{f,\overline{u}_{\theta}}and sinceu¯θ\overline{u}_{\theta}commutes withsθs_{\theta}, we deduce:∫Xϕf​(u¯θ​x)​𝑑λ​(x)=∫Xϕf,u¯θ​(x)​𝑑λ​(x)=∫XℰsθT​ϕf,u¯θ​(x)​𝑑λ​(x)+O​(|u¯θ|​T​ϵ)\displaystyle\int_{X}\phi_{f}(\overline{u}_{\theta}x)d\lambda(x)=\int_{X}\phi_{f,\overline{u}_{\theta}}(x)d\lambda(x)=\int_{X}\mathcal{E}_{s_{\theta}}^{T}\phi_{f,\overline{u}_{\theta}}(x)d\lambda(x)+O(\lvert\overline{u}_{\theta}\rvert T\epsilon)=∫XℰsθT​ϕf​(u¯θ​x)​𝑑λ​(x)+O​(|u¯θ|​T​ϵ)\displaystyle=\int_{X}\mathcal{E}_{s_{\theta}}^{T}\phi_{f}(\overline{u}_{\theta}x)d\lambda(x)+O(\lvert\overline{u}_{\theta}\rvert T\epsilon)

as desired.
∎

To recall the definition of an almost factor, we recall the one sided Hausdorff distance.

## Definition 8.2(One sided Hausdorff metric).

Given a metric space(X,d)(X,d)and setsA,B⊂XA,B\subset X, we define the one sided Hausdorff distance fromAAtoBBby:dH+​(A,B)=supa∈Ad​(a,B).d_{H}^{+}(A,B)=\sup_{a\in A}d(a,B).(8.3)

Using this definition, we can recall what an almost factor is.

## Definition 8.3(Almost factor).

LetXXbe aGG-space, letW⊂XW\subset Xbe measurable,δ>0\delta>0and let𝒬\mathcal{Q}denote the space of maximal parabolic subgroups.

We say thatWWhas a projective(R,δ)(R,\delta)-factor if there is a measurable mapπ:W→𝒬\pi:W\rightarrow\mathcal{Q}such that for everyx∈Wx\in Wwe havedH+​(stabG⁡(x)∩GR||,π​(x))≤δ.\displaystyle d_{H}^{+}(\operatorname{stab}_{G}(x)\cap G^{||}_{R},\pi(x))\leq\delta.(8.4)

WhenWWconsists of one pointxx, we simply say that the action has a projective factor atxx.

The following theorem is a step towards the full proof, where we assume that the measureλ\lambdabeing quantitative Mautner with general parameters.
Later in the final proof, we compute these parameters and drop this assumption.

## Appendix AMatrix computation

## Notation A.1.

We recall thatPPdenotes the minimal parabolic subgroup inGGand thatV¯\overline{V}denotes the unipotent radical of the opposite parabolic subgroup.

## Definition A.2.

Giveng∈Gg\in G, we say thatggiscompositeifggcan be written uniquely asg=v¯​pg=\overline{v}pfor somev¯∈V¯\overline{v}\in\overline{V}andp∈Pp\in P.

## Remark A.3.

Letθ⊂Δ\theta\subset\Delta, letVθV_{\theta}be the subgroup expanded bysθs_{\theta}and letGθ−=P¯θ=Lθ​V¯θG^{-}_{\theta}=\overline{P}_{\theta}=L_{\theta}\overline{V}_{\theta}be the corresponding non-expanded parabolic subgroup. Ifg∈V¯​P,g\in\overline{V}P,thenggadmits a unique decompositiong=gθ−​gθ+g=g^{-}_{\theta}g^{+}_{\theta}wheregθ+∈Vθg^{+}_{\theta}\in V_{\theta}andgθ−∈Gθ−g^{-}_{\theta}\in G^{-}_{\theta}.
Indeed,P=(P∩Lθ)​VθP=(P\cap L_{\theta})V_{\theta}andV¯​(P∩Lθ)⊂P¯θ\overline{V}(P\cap L_{\theta})\subset\overline{P}_{\theta}.

We will not prove the following classical fact.

## Claim A.4(Almost everything is composite).

There exists a proper submanifoldℳ⊂G\mathcal{M}\subset Gsuch that everyg∈G∖ℳg\in G\setminus\mathcal{M}is composite.
Moreover, whenG=SLn⁡(ℝ)G=\operatorname{SL}_{n}(\mathbb{R})the manifoldℳ\mathcal{M}is given explicitly byℳ={g∈SLn⁡(ℝ):det(gi​i)=0​for some​i=1,…,d−1}\mathcal{M}=\left\{g\in\operatorname{SL}_{n}(\mathbb{R}):\det(g_{ii})=0\text{ for some }i=1,\dots,d-1\right\}(A.1)

where we denotegi​i∈Mi⁡(ℝ)g_{ii}\in\operatorname{M}_{i}(\mathbb{R})to be theii’th leading principal minor ingg.

LetGGbe a simple Lie group.

andGsG^{s}be the centralizer ofss.

For everyg∈Gg\in Gthat can be written asg=g−0​g+g=g_{-0}g_{+}, forg−0∈Qg_{-0}\in Qandg+∈Vg_{+}\in Vare uniquely determined as a function ofgg.

from line to line. RecallmP,1m_{P,1}is normalised Haar measure on the
unit ballP1:=P1∥P_{1}:=P_{1}^{\|}.

Everything below rests on the following lemma. It is stated for a
family of polynomials indexed byg∈Gg\in G, which is exactly the shape in
which it will be used: the point is that the constant is polynomial in|g||g|, uniformly over the family.

## Lemma A.5.

Let{fg}g∈G\{f_{g}\}_{g\in G}be a family of polynomial functions onP1P_{1}of
degree at mostdd, whose coefficients are polynomial functions of the
matrix entries ofgg, and supposefg≢0f_{g}\not\equiv 0onP1P_{1}for everyg∈Gg\in G. Then there areC,qG>0C,q_{G}>0andn≥1n\geq 1such thatmP,1​({p∈P1:|fg​(p)|≤t})≤C​(t​|g|qG)1/nfor all​t>0,g∈G.m_{P,1}\bigl(\{p\in P_{1}:|f_{g}(p)|\leq t\}\bigr)\leq C\bigl(t\,|g|^{q_{G}}\bigr)^{1/n}\qquad\text{for all }t>0,\ g\in G.

## Proof.

Write‖f‖P1:=supP1|f|\|f\|_{P_{1}}:=\sup_{P_{1}}|f|. By the sublevel-set inequality for
polynomials of bounded degree on a compact set with nonempty interior[CarberyWright, Thm. 8]— equivalently, by the fact that such
polynomials are(C,1/d)(C,1/d)-good in the sense of[KleinbockMargulis, §3]— there areCCandnn, depending only
onddandP1P_{1}, withmP,1​({p∈P1:|f​(p)|≤ε​‖f‖P1})≤C​ε1/n,ε>0.m_{P,1}\bigl(\{p\in P_{1}:|f(p)|\leq\varepsilon\|f\|_{P_{1}}\}\bigr)\leq C\varepsilon^{1/n},\qquad\varepsilon>0.(A.2)

It therefore suffices to bound‖fg‖P1\|f_{g}\|_{P_{1}}from below byc​|g|−qGc|g|^{-q_{G}}. PutΦ​(R):=inf|g|≤R‖fg‖P1.\Phi(R):=\inf_{|g|\leq R}\|f_{g}\|_{P_{1}}.

The set{|g|≤R}\{|g|\leq R\}is compact andg↦‖fg‖P1g\mapsto\|f_{g}\|_{P_{1}}is
continuous and, by hypothesis, strictly positive; henceΦ​(R)>0\Phi(R)>0for
everyRR. MoreoverΦ\Phiis semialgebraic, by Tarski–Seidenberg
applied to the formula defining it. A positive semialgebraic function of
one variable admits a Puiseux expansion at infinity, soΦ​(R)≥c​R−qG\Phi(R)\geq cR^{-q_{G}}forR≥1R\geq 1[BCR, §2.6]. Applying
(A.2) withε=t/‖fg‖P1≤t​|g|qG/c\varepsilon=t/\|f_{g}\|_{P_{1}}\leq t|g|^{q_{G}}/cfinishes the proof.
∎

## Remark A.6.

The normalisation by‖fg‖P1\|f_{g}\|_{P_{1}}in (A.2) is not
cosmetic: rescalingfgf_{g}by a constant changes no zero set, so no bound
of the formm​({|fg|≤t})≤C​t1/nm(\{|f_{g}|\leq t\})\leq Ct^{1/n}can hold withCCindependent of the size offgf_{g}. This is the only place where the
polynomial dependence on|g||g|is produced.

## Lemma A.7.

Letg1,g2∈Gg_{1},g_{2}\in Gwithg1∈Q​Vg_{1}\in QVand putv1:=(g1)+v_{1}:=(g_{1})_{+}. Then for
everyv∈Vv\in Vfor which either side is defined,(g1​v​g2)+=((v1​v)​g2)+.(g_{1}vg_{2})_{+}=\bigl((v_{1}v)g_{2}\bigr)_{+}.

Consequentlyv⟼(g1​g2)+−1​(g1​v​g2)+v\longmapsto(g_{1}g_{2})_{+}^{-1}(g_{1}vg_{2})_{+}

is the composition of the left translationv↦v1​vv\mapsto v_{1}vofVV, the
mapρg2:u↦(u​g2)+\rho_{g_{2}}:u\mapsto(ug_{2})_{+}, and a further left translation.
Each factor is a diffeomorphism between open subsets ofVV; in
particularDg1,g2​(v0)D_{g_{1},g_{2}}(v_{0})is invertible at everyv0v_{0}at which it
is defined.

## Proof.

Writeg1=q1​v1g_{1}=q_{1}v_{1}withq1∈Qq_{1}\in Q. Theng1​v​g2=q1​(v1​v​g2)g_{1}vg_{2}=q_{1}(v_{1}vg_{2}), andQQ-factors do not affect theVV-coordinate, which gives the identity.
The mapρg2\rho_{g_{2}}is the restriction to the big cell of the rightg2g_{2}-action onQ\GQ\backslash G; its inverse isu↦(u​g2−1)+u\mapsto(ug_{2}^{-1})_{+},
sinceu​g2=q​u′ug_{2}=qu^{\prime}impliesu′​g2−1=q−1​uu^{\prime}g_{2}^{-1}=q^{-1}u.
∎

## Definition A.12.

Giveng¯=(g1,…,gn)∈Gn\overline{g}=(g_{1},\dots,g_{n})\in G^{n}we say thatg¯\overline{g}is aD,cD,c-tuple if for everyi=1,…,n−1i=1,\dots,n-1we have(g1​⋯​gi,gi+1)∈𝒟c+(g_{1}\cdots g_{i},g_{i+1})\in\mathcal{D}_{c}^{+}.

We thus omit its proof.

## Claim A.13(left stability of𝒟c+\mathcal{D}_{c}^{+}).

For everyc>0c>0and for every(g1,g2)∈𝒟c+∩(GR||)2(g_{1},g_{2})\in\mathcal{D}_{c}^{+}\cap(G_{R}^{||})^{2}there existsqG>0q_{G}>0dependent only onGGsuch that for allg∈Gc/RqGg\in G_{c/R^{q_{G}}}we have(g​g1,g2)∈𝒟c/2+(gg_{1},g_{2})\in\mathcal{D}_{c/2}^{+}.
As a result, the set ofD,cD,c-tuples is stable in the following sense(Gc/Rn​qG×⋯×Gc/Rn​qG⋅(D,c)n)∩(GR||)n⊂(D,c/2)n.\displaystyle\left(G_{c/R^{nq_{G}}}\times\cdots\times G_{c/R^{nq_{G}}}\cdot(D,c)^{n}\right)\cap(G_{R}^{||})^{n}\subset(D,c/2)^{n}.

## Claim A.14.

Given anyg1,…,gℓ∈GR||g_{1},\dots,g_{\ell}\in G_{R}^{||}andc>0c>0, we can find a subset𝒫+\mathcal{P}^{+}ofP1P_{1}measure at least1−C​R3​qG​ℓ​c1/n1-CR^{3q_{G}\ell}c^{1/n}such that for everyp∈𝒫+p\in\mathcal{P}^{+}we have thatp​g¯​p−1p\overline{g}p^{-1}is aD,cD,c-tuple.

## Proof.

By DefinitionA.12,p​g¯​p−1∈(D,c)ℓp\overline{g}p^{-1}\in(D,c)^{\ell}if and
only if(p​a​p−1,p​b​p−1)∈𝒟c+(pap^{-1},pbp^{-1})\in\mathcal{D}_{c}^{+}for each of theℓ−1\ell-1pairs(a,b)∈ℱ​(g¯):={(g1​⋯​gi,gi+1):1≤i≤ℓ−1},(a,b)\in\mathcal{F}(\overline{g}):=\bigl\{(g_{1}\cdots g_{i},\,g_{i+1}):1\leq i\leq\ell-1\bigr\},

and each such pair lies in(GRℓ∥)2(G_{R^{\ell}}^{\|})^{2}. Fix one pair and
apply ClaimA.11with the parameterx=(|a|​|b|)−qG/cx=(|a||b|)^{-q_{G}}/c:mP,1​({p∈P1:(p​a​p−1,p​b​p−1)∉𝒟c+})≤C​x−1/n=C​c1/n​(|a|​|b|)qG/n≤C​c1/n​R2​qG​ℓ/n.m_{P,1}\bigl(\{p\in P_{1}:(pap^{-1},pbp^{-1})\notin\mathcal{D}_{c}^{+}\}\bigr)\leq Cx^{-1/n}=C\,c^{1/n}(|a||b|)^{q_{G}/n}\leq C\,c^{1/n}R^{2q_{G}\ell/n}.

Summing over theℓ−1\ell-1pairs and usingℓ≤RqG​ℓ\ell\leq R^{q_{G}\ell}forR≥2R\geq 2,mP,1​({p∈P1:p​g¯​p−1∉(D,c)ℓ})≤C​R3​qG​ℓ​c1/n,m_{P,1}\bigl(\{p\in P_{1}:p\overline{g}p^{-1}\notin(D,c)^{\ell}\}\bigr)\leq CR^{3q_{G}\ell}c^{1/n},

which is the assertion.
∎

## Appendix BMarkov and Fubini computations

## Notation B.1.

For this section, we fixXXto be aGG-space and letTTbe an operator onXX.
Also, we fix a finite partitionτ\tauofXXand denote its size by#​τ\#\tau.
We denoteℰTn\mathcal{E}_{T}^{n}to be the measure on⟨T⟩\left<T\right>given by1n​∑i=0n−1δTi\tfrac{1}{n}\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\delta_{T^{i}}and moreoverℰT∓=12​n​∑i=1−nn−1δTi\mathcal{E}_{T}^{\mp}=\frac{1}{2n}\sum_{i=1-n}^{n-1}\delta_{T^{i}}.

## Definition B.2.

Denote for everyx∈Xx\in Xandm∈ℕm\in\mathbb{N}:Sm​(x)=∑k=0m−1dτ​(ℰT2k∗δx,ℰT−2k∗δx).S_{m}(x)=\sum_{k=0}^{m-1}d_{\tau}(\mathcal{E}_{T}^{2^{k}}*\delta_{x},\mathcal{E}_{T}^{-2^{k}}*\delta_{x}).(B.1)

## Corollary B.3.

The following inequality holds for everyx0∈Xx_{0}\in X:12m​|{n=0,…,2m−1:Sm​(Tn​x)>(32​log⁡#​τ)3/2}|≤(32​log⁡#​τ)−1/2.\tfrac{1}{2^{m}}\lvert\{n=0,\dots,2^{m}-1:S_{m}(T^{n}x)>(\tfrac{3}{2}\log\#\tau)^{3/2}\}\rvert\leq(\tfrac{3}{2}\log\#\tau)^{-1/2}.(B.2)

Moreover, ifSm​(Tn​x)≤(32​log⁡#​τ)3/2S_{m}(T^{n}x)\leq(\tfrac{3}{2}\log\#\tau)^{3/2}, then:1m​|{k=0,…,m−1:dτ​(ℰT2k∗δTn​x,ℰT−2k∗δTn​x)≥(3​m2​log⁡#​τ)−1/2}|≤1m1/2​(32​log⁡#​τ)2.\tfrac{1}{m}\lvert\{k=0,\dots,m-1:d_{\tau}(\mathcal{E}_{T}^{2^{k}}*\delta_{T^{n}x},\mathcal{E}_{T}^{-2^{k}}*\delta_{T^{n}x})\geq(\tfrac{3m}{2}\log\#\tau)^{-1/2}\}\rvert\leq\tfrac{1}{m^{1/2}}(\tfrac{3}{2}\log\#\tau)^{2}.(B.3)

## Proof.

Otherwise, there is a proportion of at least(32​log⁡#​τ)−1/2(\tfrac{3}{2}\log\#\tau)^{-1/2}ofnn’s between0and2m−12^{m}-1for whichSm​(Tn​x)>(32​log⁡#​τ)3/2S_{m}(T^{n}x)>(\tfrac{3}{2}\log\#\tau)^{3/2}.
This implies that:12m​∑n=02m−1Sm​(Tn​x)>(32​log⁡#​τ)3/2⋅(32​log⁡#​τ)−1/2=32​log⁡#​τ.\tfrac{1}{2^{m}}\sum_{n=0}^{2^{m}-1}S_{m}(T^{n}x)>(\tfrac{3}{2}\log\#\tau)^{3/2}\cdot(\tfrac{3}{2}\log\#\tau)^{-1/2}=\tfrac{3}{2}\log\#\tau.(B.4)

However, sinceSm​(Tn​x)S_{m}(T^{n}x)is exactly (by definition) the term in the sum appearing in the previous claim, this would contradict it.

To prove the ’moreover’ part, note that if there are at leastm1/2​(32​log⁡#​τ)2m^{1/2}(\tfrac{3}{2}\log\#\tau)^{2}numbersk=0,…,m−1k=0,\dots,m-1for whichdτ​(ℰT2k∗δTn​x,ℰT−2k∗δTn​x)≥(3​m2​log⁡#​τ)−1/2,d_{\tau}(\mathcal{E}_{T}^{2^{k}}*\delta_{T^{n}x},\mathcal{E}_{T}^{-2^{k}}*\delta_{T^{n}x})\geq(\tfrac{3m}{2}\log\#\tau)^{-1/2},(B.5)

then this implies thatSm​(Tn​x)=∑k=0m−1dτ​(ℰT2k∗δTn​x,ℰT−2k∗δTn​x)>(32​log⁡#​τ)3/2S_{m}(T^{n}x)=\sum_{k=0}^{m-1}d_{\tau}(\mathcal{E}_{T}^{2^{k}}*\delta_{T^{n}x},\mathcal{E}_{T}^{-2^{k}}*\delta_{T^{n}x})>(\tfrac{3}{2}\log\#\tau)^{3/2}(B.6)

which is once again in contradiction with the assumptionSm​(Tn​x)≤(32​log⁡#​τ)3/2S_{m}(T^{n}x)\leq(\tfrac{3}{2}\log\#\tau)^{3/2}.
∎

## Claim B.4.

Ifλ\lambdais some probability measure onXX, then there exists a numbern=0,…,2m−1n=0,\dots,2^{m}-1and a subset𝒜⊂{0,…,m−1}\mathcal{A}\subset\{0,\dots,m-1\}of proportion at least1−((32​log⁡#​τ)−1/4+1m​(32​log⁡#​τ)2)1/21-((\tfrac{3}{2}\log\#\tau)^{-1/4}+\tfrac{1}{m}(\tfrac{3}{2}\log\#\tau)^{2})^{1/2}such that for everyk∈𝒜k\in\mathcal{A}there exists a subsetX0​(k)⊂XX_{0}(k)\subset Xofλ\lambda-measure at least1−((32​log⁡#​τ)−1/4+1m​(32​log⁡#​τ)2)1/21-((\tfrac{3}{2}\log\#\tau)^{-1/4}+\tfrac{1}{m}(\tfrac{3}{2}\log\#\tau)^{2})^{1/2}such that for everyx∈X0​(k)x\in X_{0}(k)we have:dτ​(ℰT2k∗δTn​x,ℰT−2k∗δTn​x)≤(3​m2​log⁡#​τ)−1/2.d_{\tau}(\mathcal{E}_{T}^{2^{k}}*\delta_{T^{n}x},\mathcal{E}_{T}^{-2^{k}}*\delta_{T^{n}x})\leq(\tfrac{3m}{2}\log\#\tau)^{-1/2}.(B.7)

## Proof.

We will use CorollaryB.3and Fubini Theorem.
Denote for every natural numberℓ\ellthe measurem[ℓ]m_{[\ell]}as the uniform probability measure on{0,…,ℓ−1}\{0,\dots,\ell-1\}.
Then by the first part of the corollary and by Fubini we have:m[2m]×λ​({(n,x)∈[2m−1]×X:Sm​(Tn​x)>(32​log⁡#​τ)3/2})≤(32​log⁡#​τ)−1/2.m_{[2^{m}]}\times\lambda(\{(n,x)\in[2^{m}-1]\times X:S_{m}(T^{n}x)>(\tfrac{3}{2}\log\#\tau)^{3/2}\})\leq(\tfrac{3}{2}\log\#\tau)^{-1/2}.(B.8)

Again by Fubini, this implies:∫[2m−1]λ​({Sm​(Tn​x)>(32​log⁡#​τ)3/2})​𝑑m[2m]​(n)≤(32​log⁡#​τ)−1/2.\int_{[2^{m}-1]}\lambda(\{S_{m}(T^{n}x)>(\tfrac{3}{2}\log\#\tau)^{3/2}\})dm_{[2^{m}]}(n)\leq(\tfrac{3}{2}\log\#\tau)^{-1/2}.(B.9)

By Markov inequality we deduce that:m[2m]​({λ​({Sm​(Tn​x)>(32​log⁡#​τ)3/2})>(32​log⁡#​τ)−1/4})≤(32​log⁡#​τ)−1/4m_{[2^{m}]}(\{\lambda(\{S_{m}(T^{n}x)>(\tfrac{3}{2}\log\#\tau)^{3/2}\})>(\tfrac{3}{2}\log\#\tau)^{-1/4}\})\leq(\tfrac{3}{2}\log\#\tau)^{-1/4}(B.10)

which says, in other words, that there exists a subset𝒜′⊂[2m−1]\mathcal{A}^{\prime}\subset[2^{m}-1]of measure at least(32​log⁡#​τ)−1/4(\tfrac{3}{2}\log\#\tau)^{-1/4}such that for eachn∈𝒜′n\in\mathcal{A}^{\prime}we haveλ​({Sm​(Tn​x)>(32​log⁡#​τ)3/2})≤(32​log⁡#​τ)−1/4\lambda(\{S_{m}(T^{n}x)>(\tfrac{3}{2}\log\#\tau)^{3/2}\})\leq(\tfrac{3}{2}\log\#\tau)^{-1/4}.
In particular, take somen∈𝒜′n\in\mathcal{A}^{\prime}.
DenoteX0={x∈X:Sm​(Tn​x)≤(32​log⁡#​τ)3/2}X_{0}=\{x\in X:S_{m}(T^{n}x)\leq(\tfrac{3}{2}\log\#\tau)^{3/2}\}.
Then we have proved thatλ​(X0)≥1−(32​log⁡#​τ)−1/4\lambda(X_{0})\geq 1-(\tfrac{3}{2}\log\#\tau)^{-1/4}.
By the ’moreover’ part of CorollaryB.3, since for everyx∈X0x\in X_{0}we haveSm​(Tn​x)≤(32​log⁡#​τ)3/2S_{m}(T^{n}x)\leq(\tfrac{3}{2}\log\#\tau)^{3/2}, we know that:m[m]​({k∈[m−1]:dτ​(ℰT2k∗δTn​x,ℰT−2k∗δTn​x)≥(3​m2​log⁡#​τ)−1/2})≤1m1/2​(32​log⁡#​τ)2.m_{[m]}(\{k\in[m-1]:d_{\tau}(\mathcal{E}_{T}^{2^{k}}*\delta_{T^{n}x},\mathcal{E}_{T}^{-2^{k}}*\delta_{T^{n}x})\geq(\tfrac{3m}{2}\log\#\tau)^{-1/2}\})\leq\tfrac{1}{m^{1/2}}(\tfrac{3}{2}\log\#\tau)^{2}.(B.11)

Sinceλ​(X0)≥1−(32​log⁡#​τ)−1/4\lambda(X_{0})\geq 1-(\tfrac{3}{2}\log\#\tau)^{-1/4}we can deduce:m[m]×λ​({(k,x):dτ​(ℰT2k∗δTn​x,ℰT−2k∗δTn​x)≥(3​m2​log⁡#​τ)−1/2})≤(32​log⁡#​τ)−1/4+1m1/2​(32​log⁡#​τ)2.m_{[m]}\times\lambda(\{(k,x):d_{\tau}(\mathcal{E}_{T}^{2^{k}}*\delta_{T^{n}x},\mathcal{E}_{T}^{-2^{k}}*\delta_{T^{n}x})\geq(\tfrac{3m}{2}\log\#\tau)^{-1/2}\})\leq(\tfrac{3}{2}\log\#\tau)^{-1/4}+\tfrac{1}{m^{1/2}}(\tfrac{3}{2}\log\#\tau)^{2}.(B.12)

By Fubini, this shows that∫[m−1]λ​({x∈X:dτ​(ℰT2k∗δTn​x,ℰT−2k∗δTn​x)≥(3​m2​log⁡#​τ)−1/2})​𝑑m[m]​(k)\displaystyle\int_{[m-1]}\lambda(\{x\in X:d_{\tau}(\mathcal{E}_{T}^{2^{k}}*\delta_{T^{n}x},\mathcal{E}_{T}^{-2^{k}}*\delta_{T^{n}x})\geq(\tfrac{3m}{2}\log\#\tau)^{-1/2}\})dm_{[m]}(k)≤(32​log⁡#​τ)−1/4+1m1/2​(32​log⁡#​τ)2.\displaystyle\leq(\tfrac{3}{2}\log\#\tau)^{-1/4}+\tfrac{1}{m^{1/2}}(\tfrac{3}{2}\log\#\tau)^{2}.

By Markov again, we obtain:m[m]({k:λ({x∈X:dτ(ℰT2k∗δTn​x,ℰT−2k∗δTn​x)≥(3​m2log#τ)−1/2})\displaystyle m_{[m]}(\{k:\lambda(\{x\in X:d_{\tau}(\mathcal{E}_{T}^{2^{k}}*\delta_{T^{n}x},\mathcal{E}_{T}^{-2^{k}}*\delta_{T^{n}x})\geq(\tfrac{3m}{2}\log\#\tau)^{-1/2}\})≥((32log#τ)−1/4+1m1/2(32log#τ)2)1/2})≤((32log#τ)−1/4+1m1/2(32log#τ)2)1/2\displaystyle\geq((\tfrac{3}{2}\log\#\tau)^{-1/4}+\tfrac{1}{m^{1/2}}(\tfrac{3}{2}\log\#\tau)^{2})^{1/2}\})\leq((\tfrac{3}{2}\log\#\tau)^{-1/4}+\tfrac{1}{m^{1/2}}(\tfrac{3}{2}\log\#\tau)^{2})^{1/2}

and therefore there exists a subset𝒜⊂[m−1]\mathcal{A}\subset[m-1]of measure at least1−((32​log⁡#​τ)−1/4+1m1/2​(32​log⁡#​τ)2)1/21-((\tfrac{3}{2}\log\#\tau)^{-1/4}+\tfrac{1}{m^{1/2}}(\tfrac{3}{2}\log\#\tau)^{2})^{1/2}such that for everyk∈𝒜k\in\mathcal{A}, we have:λ​({x∈X:dτ​(ℰT2k∗δTn​x,ℰT−2k∗δTn​x)≥(3​m2​log⁡#​τ)−1/2})≤((32​log⁡#​τ)−1/4+1m1/2​(32​log⁡#​τ)2)1/2\lambda(\{x\in X:d_{\tau}(\mathcal{E}_{T}^{2^{k}}*\delta_{T^{n}x},\mathcal{E}_{T}^{-2^{k}}*\delta_{T^{n}x})\geq(\tfrac{3m}{2}\log\#\tau)^{-1/2}\})\leq((\tfrac{3}{2}\log\#\tau)^{-1/4}+\tfrac{1}{m^{1/2}}(\tfrac{3}{2}\log\#\tau)^{2})^{1/2}(B.13)

namely for everyk∈𝒜k\in\mathcal{A}there existsX0​(k)⊂XX_{0}(k)\subset Xof measure at least1−((32​log⁡#​τ)−1/4+1m1/2​(32​log⁡#​τ)2)1/21-((\tfrac{3}{2}\log\#\tau)^{-1/4}+\tfrac{1}{m^{1/2}}(\tfrac{3}{2}\log\#\tau)^{2})^{1/2}such that for everyx∈X0​(k)x\in X_{0}(k)we have:dτ​(ℰT2k∗δTn​x,ℰT−2k∗δTn​x)≤(3​m2​log⁡#​τ)−1/2d_{\tau}(\mathcal{E}_{T}^{2^{k}}*\delta_{T^{n}x},\mathcal{E}_{T}^{-2^{k}}*\delta_{T^{n}x})\leq(\tfrac{3m}{2}\log\#\tau)^{-1/2}(B.14)

as desired.
∎

## Appendix CUpgrade to parabolic

Recall thatGGis simple with finite centre, thats∈𝔞+∖{0}s\in\mathfrak{a}^{+}\setminus\{0\}is a singular direction, and thatVVis the expanding horospherical
subgroup forss; equivalentlyQ+:=Gs​VQ^{+}:=G^{s}Vis the parabolic subgroup not
contracted byssandV=Ru​(Q+)V=R_{u}(Q^{+}). Only the last property is used
below, so we state the result for the unipotent radical of an arbitrary
proper parabolic. SinceQ+≠GQ^{+}\neq Gwe haveV≠{e}V\neq\{e\}.

We regardGGas the group of real points of a linear algebraic group,
which is legitimate becauseZ​(G)Z(G)is finite.

## Theorem C.1.

LetQ+<GQ^{+}<Gbe a proper parabolic subgroup andV=Ru​(Q+)V=R_{u}(Q^{+}). Then every
proper closed subgroupH<GH<GcontainingVVis contained in a maximal
parabolic subgroup ofGG.

The two classical inputs are the following. First, ifL<GL<Gis a
reductive algebraic subgroup, then some Cartan involution ofGGpreservesLL[Mostow55]. Second, ifU<GU<Gis a nontrivial
unipotent subgroup, then there is a proper parabolic subgroupP<GP<GwithU⊂Ru​(P)U\subset R_{u}(P)andNG​(U)⊂PN_{G}(U)\subset P[BorelTits71, Cor. 3.2];
see also[MalleTesterman, Rem. 17.16].

## Lemma C.2.

LetQ+<GQ^{+}<Gbe a proper parabolic subgroup,U+=Ru​(Q+)U^{+}=R_{u}(Q^{+}), and letϑ\varthetabe any Cartan involution ofGG. Thenϑ​(Q+)\vartheta(Q^{+})is the
parabolic subgroup opposite toQ+Q^{+}, andU+U^{+}together withU−:=ϑ​(U+)U^{-}:=\vartheta(U^{+})generatesGG.

Letθ\thetaalso denote the involution of𝔤\mathfrak{g}, letK=GϑK=G^{\vartheta},
and fix a minimal parabolicP0P_{0}built from aϑ\vartheta-stable Cartan
subspace, so thatϑ​(𝔮0)=𝔮0−\vartheta(\mathfrak{q}_{0})=\mathfrak{q}_{0}^{-}for every standard
parabolic subalgebra𝔮0⊃𝔭0\mathfrak{q}_{0}\supset\mathfrak{p}_{0}. Write𝔮+=Ad⁡(g)​𝔮0\mathfrak{q}^{+}=\operatorname{Ad}(g)\mathfrak{q}_{0}for a suitable standard𝔮0\mathfrak{q}_{0}andg∈Gg\in G, andg=k​pg=kpwithk∈Kk\in K,p∈P0⊂Q0p\in P_{0}\subset Q_{0}by the Iwasawa
decomposition. Then𝔮+=Ad⁡(k)​𝔮0\mathfrak{q}^{+}=\operatorname{Ad}(k)\mathfrak{q}_{0}, and sinceϑ\varthetafixeskk,ϑ​(𝔮+)=Ad⁡(k)​ϑ​(𝔮0)=Ad⁡(k)​𝔮0−,\vartheta(\mathfrak{q}^{+})=\operatorname{Ad}(k)\,\vartheta(\mathfrak{q}_{0})=\operatorname{Ad}(k)\mathfrak{q}_{0}^{-},

which is opposite toAd⁡(k)​𝔮0=𝔮+\operatorname{Ad}(k)\mathfrak{q}_{0}=\mathfrak{q}^{+}. This proves the first
assertion, and gives the decomposition𝔤=𝔲−⊕𝔩⊕𝔲+\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{u}^{-}\oplus\mathfrak{l}\oplus\mathfrak{u}^{+}, where𝔩\mathfrak{l}is the commonϑ\vartheta-stable Levi factor.

Let𝔥\mathfrak{h}be the subalgebra generated by𝔲+\mathfrak{u}^{+}and𝔲−\mathfrak{u}^{-}. It is
stable underad​𝔲±\mathrm{ad}\mathfrak{u}^{\pm}because it is a subalgebra containing𝔲±\mathfrak{u}^{\pm}. It is stable underad​𝔩\mathrm{ad}\mathfrak{l}because𝔩\mathfrak{l}normalises𝔲+\mathfrak{u}^{+}and𝔲−\mathfrak{u}^{-}, hence normalises the subalgebra they generate, by the

connected subgroup with Lie algebra𝔤\mathfrak{g}, hence open, hence equal toGG.
